- [62] `line:570` `kind:user_query_tag` Make targeted visual refinements to the Nutrition Facts screen in CoachConnect. Do not change any layout, structure, or functionality. Only adjust colors, opacity, spacing, and typography as listed below. --- ## 1. Tone down accent colors throughout The current colors are too saturated and feel aggressive. Replace these values everywhere they appear on this screen: Pink: #FF6B9D → #E85D8A Orange: #F97316 → #D4621A Cyan: #64D2FF → #4DB8E8 Purple: #C084FC → #A970E8 Apply these replacements to: section header labels, progress bar fills, macro card labels, gradient accent lines, and any colored text values. Do NOT change the colors on the bottom nav bar or anywhere outside this screen. --- ## 2. Gradient accent lines on cards The top border gradient lines on the header card and section cards are too thick and bright. Change all gradient accent lines to: - Height: 1px (from whatever they currently are) - Opacity: 0.6 - Keep the same gradient direction and colors (just muted per change above) --- ## 3. Section header labels (FATS, CARBOHYDRATES, etc.) Currently too loud. Make these feel like labels not headlines. - Font size: 11px (reduce from current) - Letter spacing: 1.5px - Opacity: 0.7 (not full white or full color) - Keep the color (cyan for FATS, orange for CARBS, pink for VITAMINS) but at 0.7 opacity --- ## 4. Macro cards — more breathing room Increase internal padding on each of the three macro cards from current value to 16px on all sides. The gram value (e.g. "34.9g") — increase font size to 26px bold if currently smaller than that. The "X / Xg" goal text below the bar — reduce to 10px and set opacity to 0.5 so it reads as supporting info not primary data. --- ## 5. Header card calorie display The "CAL" label next to the large calorie number: - Reduce CAL to 11px - Set CAL opacity to 0.5 - Add 4px margin between the number and CAL label - The large calorie number itself: keep size but change color to #E85D8A (muted pink per change above) --- ## 6. Card background depth Currently all cards blend together against #0A0A0F. For every other glass card on this screen (alternating): Add background: rgba(255, 255, 255, 0.02) on top of whatever background they already have. This adds just enough depth to separate cards visually without adding visible borders or changing the dark feel. --- ## 7. Progress bars All progress bars on this screen: - Reduce height from current to 5px - Border radius: 3px - Track color: rgba(255, 255, 255, 0.06) (slightly more muted than current) - Keep gradient fills but use muted colors from change 1 --- ## 8. Row values that are 0g or unavailable Any value showing 0g, 0mg, or — on this screen: Set that text to opacity 0.35 instead of full white. This makes non-zero values stand out naturally without any extra styling. --- ## Do not change - Layout or structure of any section - Font family - The bottom nav bar - Any data fetching or state logic - Card border radius - Section order - Scrolling behavior

Based on the results, the clearest match is that vague questions are weak because they’re too broad to act on, and the fix is to make them tied to actual content or ask for a concrete “muddiest point” instead of “any questions?”[2][3] Here’s the specific stuff I found: The Byrdseed article says questions like “How do you feel about the characters?” are too abstract because they don’t connect to a specific book or moment, so they don’t help much.[2] It gives better examples like “Who do you think was bravest out of Dorothy’s group?” and “If Dorothy had to leave one friend behind, who would it be?” because those force an actual, grounded answer.[2] The Faculty Focus piece says “Any questions?” and “Is everybody with me?” usually get silence and can even signal that you want people to stop talking.[3] Its concrete replacement is the “muddiest point” technique, where people say exactly what is still confusing, which gives you a much clearer read on what they don’t get.[3] So the exact pattern is this: vague prompts get vague or no answers, and specific prompts get usable answers.[2][3] If you want, I can turn your question into a sharper version right now and show you what a good specific prompt looks like.can u make the damn ai coach stop doing that pls

Replace it with nothing — let the screen breathe with just the header and then go straight into the CAN HELP WITH section. If removing it breaks any layout, replace it with just 24px of vertical spacing. --- ## CAN HELP WITH cards Each card in this carousel: - Glass card background: rgba(255,255,255,0.05) - Border: 1px solid rgba(255,255,255,0.08) - Remove the current orange/red border completely - Add a subtle pink → purple gradient top border accent line: 1.5px, full width, at the very top of the card, opacity 0.8 - Border radius: 16px - Padding: 16px Card layout (horizontal row): - Left: icon container - 44x44 rounded square (border radius 10px) - Background: rgba of the accent color at 15% opacity - Icon: Ionicons, 22px, colored to match accent - Each card gets a different accent color cycling through pink, purple, cyan, orange - Right of icon: text block - Title: white, 16px, semibold - Subtitle: muted (#888), 13px, below title Carousel dots: - Active dot: pink #FF6B9D, 8px wide, 4px tall, pill shape (border radius 2px) - Inactive dots: rgba(255,255,255,0.2), 4px circle - Center aligned below the cards - 6px gap between dots --- ## WHAT DO YOU NEED section The four circle buttons (Train, Fuel, Recover, Web): - Remove the gold/yellow borders entirely - Circle background: rgba(255,255,255,0.06) - Border: 1px solid rgba(255,255,255,0.1) - Each circle gets a gradient border using the accent colors — use a thin colored ring approach: Train → pink ring Fuel → orange ring Recover → purple ring Web → cyan ring - Icon size: increase to 26px inside the circle - Label below: 12px, muted white, same color as the ring Colored ring implementation: Wrap each circle in a slightly larger container with a gradient background, then the inner circle sits on top with a 1.5px gap — creates the illusion of a colored gradient border. --- ## TRY ASKING cards Each card in this carousel: - Same glass card style as CAN HELP WITH above - Remove the current orange/red border - Add a subtle gradient top border accent: purple → pink, 1.5px, opacity 0.8 - Border radius: 16px - Padding: 16px Card layout: - Left: icon container - Same 44x44 rounded square style - Background: rgba(255,107,157,0.12) — subtle pink - Icon: relevant Ionicons for the question topic, 22px, pink #FF6B9D - Center: question text - White, 15px, semibold, flex:1 - Max 2 lines, ellipsis after - Right: arrow button - 36x36 circle - Background: pink → purple gradient (#FF6B9D → #C084FC) - Ionicons chevron-forward, white, 18px - Remove the current dark red circle entirely Carousel dots: same style as CAN HELP WITH section. --- ## Section labels (CAN HELP WITH, TRY ASKING) - Font size: 11px - Letter spacing: 1.5px - Color: rgba(255,255,255,0.4) - All caps - No changes to position --- ## Do not touch - The input bar at the bottom (Ask your coach...) - The History button in the header - The person and settings icons in the header - The bottom nav bar - Any chat logic or data - The WHAT DO YOU NEED section layout (only update colors per above)

Critical logic that should have unit tests - Pure functions that calculate or transform data (macro calculations, date formatting, role normalization, tool call parsing, food search scoring, etc.) - Any function where a bug would directly cause wrong data shown to users (wrong calories, wrong macros, wrong role routing) - Validation functions (form validation, input checks) ### 2. Critical flows that should have integration tests - The auth routing flow (trainer vs client vs onboarding) - The food search flow (query → results → log) - The AI Coach message flow (send → receive → tool call) - The workout generation flow (generate → save → display) - The trainer-client link flow (invite code → link created) - Any flow where a silent failure would hurt users ### 3. Components that should have render tests - Components that show different UI based on data state (empty state vs loaded state vs error state) - Components with conditional rendering based on user role - Components that handle user input and validation - Any component where broken rendering would be immediately visible to users ### 4. Known bugs and silent failures from the codebase - Look for empty catch blocks that swallow errors - Look for functions that return null or undefined silently - Look for places where missing data causes no error but shows wrong UI - These are the highest priority to test ### 5. What NOT to test - Simple display-only components with no logic - Third party library code - Styling --- ## What to give me back For each item found: 1. What to test — the function, component, or flow name and exact file path 2. Why it matters — what breaks if this has a bug 3. What type of test — unit, integration, or render test 4. Example test cases to cover — specific inputs and expected outputs or behaviors 5. Difficulty — easy, medium, or hard to test At the end give me a ranked list of the top 15 most important things to test in order of priority, with a one sentence explanation of why each one is on the list. Do NOT write any test code. Read only and report.

- [13] `line:116` `kind:user_query_tag` Fix the following confirmed bugs in CoachConnect found by the Jest test suite. Read each file completely before making any changes. Fix only what is listed. Do not refactor anything else. --- ## Fix 1 — validateTrainerCode fail-open File: src/auth/trainerCodeValidation.js Lines around 50-55 THE BUG: The catch block sets codeValid: true when the API throws a network error or returns a 500. This means a broken server lets anyone bypass trainer code validation. Current broken code pattern: } catch (error) { console.warn('Trainer code validation failed...', error) setCodeValid(true) ← THIS IS WRONG setCodeError(null) } THE FIX: In the catch block, set codeValid to FALSE not true. Set a user-facing error message instead of null. Never silently pass validation on a network failure. Fixed pattern: } catch (error) { console.warn('Trainer code validation failed...', error) setCodeValid(false) setCodeError('Could not verify trainer code. Please check your connection and try again.') } After fixing run: npm run test:coverage -- --testPathPattern=trainerCodeValidation All 8 tests must pass including the two that were previously failing. --- ## Fix 2 — Food log date key NaN-NaN-NaN File: src/nutrition/services/nutritionService.js Find the function that builds the date key for food logs (the function that creates YYYY-MM-DD strings for Firestore) THE BUG: Passing a malformed or invalid date string produces "NaN-NaN-NaN" as the date key which silently writes to the wrong Firestore document and the log is lost. THE FIX: Before building the date key string, validate the date: - If the input is not a valid Date object or valid date string, do NOT proceed with the write - Throw a clear error: throw new Error('Invalid date passed to food log: ' + input) - OR normalize it to today's date using getLocalDateKey() with a console.warn so it's visible in logs Choose whichever approach is already consistent with how the rest of the file handles invalid inputs. Do not change valid date paths — only add the guard for invalid/NaN cases. After fixing run: npm run test:coverage -- --testPathPattern=foodSearchLog All 6 tests must pass. --- ## Fix 3 — LogNutritionModal reads wrong param key File: src/aiChat/toolModals/LogNutritionModal.jsx THE BUG: The modal reads params.food or params.foodName to display the food name but the tool call sends the food name as params.name. This causes the modal to show "Food" instead of the actual food name like "Big Mac". THE FIX: Find where the food name is read from params in this file. Change it to read params.name first, then fall back to params.food or params.foodName if params.name is missing: const foodName = params.name || params.food || params.foodName || 'Food item' Apply the same fallback pattern to the calories display if it has the same issue reading the wrong key. After fixing run: npm run test:coverage -- --testPathPattern=ToolConfirmationModal All 7 tests must pass including the logNutrition test. --- ## Fix 4 — BMR crashes on missing gender File: src/app/calculations.js Function: calculateBMR THE BUG: When gender is undefined or null, the function calls .toLowerCase() on it and throws: TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'toLowerCase') THE FIX: Add a guard at the top of calculateBMR before calling .toLowerCase() on gender: if (!gender) { console.warn('calculateBMR: missing gender, defaulting to male') gender = 'male' } OR throw a clear error if a default is not appropriate: if (!gender) { throw new Error('calculateBMR requires a gender value') } Choose whichever is consistent with how the rest of the onboarding flow handles missing fields. Do not change the BMR formula itself. --- ## Fix 5 — BMI returns Infinity on zero height File: src/app/calculations.js Function: calculateBMI THE BUG: When height is 0, the function divides by zero and returns Infinity instead of null or throwing. THE FIX: Add a guard before the division: if (!height || height { jest.clearAllTimers() }) - afterAll(() => { jest.useRealTimers() }) - If any mock intervals or subscriptions are created, make sure they are cleared in afterEach or afterAll Do not change any test assertions. All 19 tests must still pass after cleanup. After fixing run: npm run test:coverage -- --testPathPattern=authGate Confirm 19 tests pass AND the async warning is gone. --- ## After all fixes run the full suite Run this to confirm nothing broke across all 15 tests: npm test All 241 tests must pass.

- [18] `line:221` `kind:user_query_tag` Do a final pre-launch audit of CoachConnect. Read the entire codebase. Do not change anything. Report only. Focus exclusively on things that would break the app or hurt real users on launch day. --- ## What to look for ### 1. Authentication and access control - Can a client access trainer data or vice versa - Can an unauthenticated user reach any protected route - Are Firestore security rules enforcing role separation - Can a trainer see another trainer's clients - Any route or screen that skips auth checks ### 2. Payment and billing integrity - Can a trainer access premium features without paying - Can the 5% platform fee be bypassed - Is Stripe webhook handling idempotent (duplicate events won't charge twice) - What happens if a subscription lapses mid-session - Is the trainer's Stripe Connect account required before clients can pay them ### 3. Data integrity on write - Any Firestore write that could corrupt the 3-surface trainer-client link (users.trainerId + trainer_clients + trainer_client_links) - Any place where a partial write could leave the database in an inconsistent state - Any missing transaction where one should exist - Can a client be linked to two trainers simultaneously ### 4. AI Coach safety - Can the AI Coach execute a tool that deletes data without a confirmation modal - Is the 3 generations per user per month limit enforced server-side not just client-side - Can trainer mode be bypassed to write client data - Is there any prompt injection risk in the user message pipeline ### 5. Rate limiting and abuse prevention - Can a user spam the AI Coach endpoint - Can a user spam the food search endpoint - Is the workout generation limit enforced at the server level - Are API keys exposed anywhere in the client bundle - Can the Serper or DeepSeek budget caps be exceeded by a single user ### 6. iOS App Store compliance - Any use of private Apple APIs - Any screen that collects data Apple requires disclosure for (health, location, contacts) - Is the privacy manifest up to date - Any missing permission descriptions in Info.plist - Any feature that requires a capability not declared - Any hard-coded App Store rejection risks ### 7. Crash risks on first launch - Any screen that assumes data exists before onboarding is complete - Any null/undefined access on first open with no existing Firestore data - Any missing loading state that would show broken UI before data arrives - Any useEffect that fires before auth is confirmed ### 8. Network and offline handling - What happens when the app opens with no internet - What happens when a request fails mid-onboarding - Are there any infinite loading states with no timeout - Is there any state that gets stuck if a push notification arrives while the app is loading ### 9. Push notification tokens - Is the push token saved before the first notification is sent - What happens if the token save fails - Is there a race condition between auth completing and the token being registered ### 10. Environment and config - Are all required env vars documented and present - Is anything hardcoded that should be an env var - Does the production build point to production Firebase and Cloud Run not dev/staging - Are Stripe keys set to live not test in production --- ## How to report For each issue found: - File and line number - What breaks for the user in plain English - Severity: LAUNCH BLOCKER, HIGH, or MEDIUM - One sentence fix Only report LAUNCH BLOCKER and HIGH severity issues. Ignore anything that is a nice-to-have or post-launch fix. Do not report anything already covered by existing Jest tests in src/__tests__/. End with a ranked list of everything that must be fixed before the app goes live, ordered by severity.

- [20] `line:265` `kind:user_query_tag` Fix the following Firestore and Storage security rule issues in CoachConnect. Read firestore.rules and storage.rules completely before changing anything. Do not touch any app code — rules files only. --- ## Fix 1 — Block clients from self-assigning trainerId File: firestore.rules Location: users/{userId} write rules Currently a client can update their own users doc and set any trainerId they want. This lets them read any trainer's client health data. Fix: In the users/{userId} update rule, explicitly disallow changes to the trainerId, role, and linked fields from the client side: Add this condition to the update rule: !request.resource.data.diff(resource.data).affectedKeys() .hasAny(['trainerId', 'role', 'linked']) Clients can still update their own profile (name, weight, goals etc) but cannot change their trainer assignment directly. --- ## Fix 2 — Use CRM as single source of truth for trainer access File: firestore.rules Location: dailyLogs, nutrition_logs, weeklySummaries read rules (lines 661-666, 688-693) Currently these collections allow trainer read if: get(/users/$(userId)).data.trainerId == request.auth.uid This is inconsistent with workoutPlan and progressPhotos which use trainer_clients CRM. Fix: Add a helper function at the top of the rules: function isLinkedTrainer(userId) { return exists(/databases/$(database)/documents/ trainer_clients/$(request.auth.uid)/ clients/$(userId)); } Use isLinkedTrainer(userId) consistently across ALL trainer read rules — dailyLogs, nutrition, weeklySummaries, workoutPlan, progressPhotos. Remove standalone trainerId-only checks. --- ## Fix 3 — Block trainers from adding arbitrary clients to CRM File: firestore.rules Location: trainer_clients/{trainerUid}/clients/{clientUid} create rule (around line 373-374) Currently allows any trainer to create a CRM entry for any client UID with no validation. Fix: Remove client-side create permission entirely: allow create: if false; The server Admin SDK bypasses rules and can still write CRM entries. No client-side code should be creating these directly. --- ## Fix 4 — Require auth for profile photo reads File: storage.rules Location: profile_photos/{userId} read rule (around line 37-38) Change from: allow read: if true To: allow read: if request.auth != null --- ## Fix 5 — Lock down notification creates File: firestore.rules Location: notifications collection create rule (around lines 559-561) Currently: allow create: if request.auth != null Fix: Restrict to server-only: allow create: if false; Server Admin SDK bypasses this and can still create notifications. Clients should never create notifications directly. --- After all rule changes: 1. Review the full rules file to confirm no unintended access paths were opened or closed 2. Confirm trainer read access still works via the isLinkedTrainer CRM function 3. Report any rule that could not be fixed cleanly with an explanation of why Do not deploy. Just update the rules files. Run npm test after to confirm nothing broke.
- [21] `line:281` `kind:user_query_tag` There are 4 issues that must be fixed before firestore.rules can be safely deployed. Read every file completely before changing anything. --- ## Fix 1 — Block trainerId on users create File: firebase/firestore.rules Location: users/{userId} create rule Fix 1 already blocks trainerId on updates. Apply the same field guard to the create rule: !request.resource.data.keys() .hasAny(['trainerId', 'role', 'linked']) This prevents a new account from setting these fields on first write. --- ## Fix 2 — Move TrainerMarketplaceModal CRM writes to server File: src/trainer/dashboard/TrainerMarketplaceModal.js Location: lines 93-223 (handleAddClient function) THE BUG: This file batch-writes directly to: - trainer_client_links/{trainerId}_{clientId} - trainer_clients/{trainerId}/clients/{clientId} - users/{clientId} After deploying the new rules these writes will fail because trainer_clients create is now server-only. Fix: Replace the client-side batch writes with a server API call: POST /api/trainer/accept-client Body: { trainerId, clientId, clientData } Create this endpoint in server/routes/trainerRoutes.js (or the most appropriate existing routes file). The endpoint should: 1. Verify the request is from an authenticated trainer 2. Verify the trainer owns the trainerId in the request 3. Verify a pending messages doc exists with senderId === clientId, recipientId === trainerId, status === 'pending', requestType === 'connection' 4. Use Admin SDK to write all 3 surfaces: - trainer_client_links/{trainerId}_{clientId} - trainer_clients/{trainerId}/clients/{clientId} - users/{clientId} merge { trainerId } 5. Update the message status to 'accepted' 6. Return { success: true } In TrainerMarketplaceModal.js replace the batch write with a fetch call to this endpoint using the existing getApiAuthHeaders() pattern. --- ## Fix 3 — Move dashboard notifications to server File: src/client/screens/MyDashboardScreen.jsx Location: lines 385-393 (addDoc on notifications) THE BUG: MyDashboardScreen creates notification documents directly via addDoc. After deploying the new rules this will fail silently. Fix: Replace the direct addDoc call with a server API call: POST /api/notifications/create Body: { recipientId: trainerId, type: 'dashboard_update', clientId, payload: notificationData } Create this endpoint in server/routes/notificationsRoutes.js. The endpoint uses Admin SDK to create the notification. Verify the sender is authenticated and has an active CRM link to the recipient before creating. In MyDashboardScreen.jsx replace the addDoc call with a fetch to this endpoint. --- ## Fix 4 — Fix the failing dashboardSave test File: src/__tests__/integration/dashboardSave.test.js One test is currently failing after the rules changes. Read the test file and the error output completely. Fix the test mock to match the current Firestore write pattern after the rules changes. Do not change the actual app code — only the test. --- After all fixes run: npm test All 422 tests must pass with 0 failures. Then report that the rules are safe to deploy. firebase deploy --only firestore:rules,storage
- [22] `line:308` `kind:user_query_tag` Fix the following app-side issues in CoachConnect. Read every file completely before changing anything. Do NOT touch any payment screens or Stripe code. --- ## Fix 1 — Fix push token save on new accounts File: src/shared/services/notificationsService.js Location: lines 156-185 THE BUG: Uses updateDoc which fails silently if the user doc doesn't exist yet. New users never receive push notifications. Fix Part A: Change updateDoc to setDoc with merge: await setDoc( doc(db, 'users', uid), { expoPushToken: token, pushTokenUpdatedAt: now }, { merge: true } ); Fix Part B: Add retry on failure: try { await setDoc(userRef, tokenPayload, { merge: true }); } catch (error) { console.warn('Push token save failed, retrying on next launch:', error.message); await AsyncStorage.setItem( `pending_push_token_${uid}`, token ); } Fix Part C: On app launch check for pending token. In ClientApp.js and TrainerApp.js after confirming user doc exists add: const pendingToken = await AsyncStorage.getItem( `pending_push_token_${uid}` ); if (pendingToken) { await persistPushTokensForUid(uid, pendingToken); await AsyncStorage.removeItem( `pending_push_token_${uid}` ); } --- ## Fix 2 — Privacy manifest data types File: ios/CoachConnect/PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy Location: NSPrivacyCollectedDataTypes (lines 43-44) THE BUG: Empty array. App Store will reject this. Fix: Replace the empty NSPrivacyCollectedDataTypes array with the correct entries: - NSPrivacyCollectedDataTypeHealth (sleep, steps, calories, water, mood) - NSPrivacyCollectedDataTypeFitnessAndExercise (workout logs, active workouts) - NSPrivacyCollectedDataTypePhotosAndVideos (progress photos) - NSPrivacyCollectedDataTypeOtherUserContent (notes, messages, AI chat history) - NSPrivacyCollectedDataTypeDeviceID (push notification token) - NSPrivacyCollectedDataTypeName (first name, last name) - NSPrivacyCollectedDataTypeEmailAddress (account email) For each entry set: - NSPrivacyCollectedDataTypeLinked: true - NSPrivacyCollectedDataTypeTracking: false - NSPrivacyCollectedDataTypePurposes: [NSPrivacyCollectedDataTypePurposeAppFunctionality] --- After all fixes run: npm test All existing tests must still pass. Report any test that breaks and why. Also manually verify on a real device: 1. New user completes onboarding and receives a push notification on first session invite 2. A client can browse the marketplace tap Connect and the trainer sees the request in their Client Requests tab 3. Trainer accepts and client home screen updates to show the trainer card

File to create: src/__tests__/unit/dataCacheCleanup.test.js Read this file completely before writing: - src/utils/dataCacheCleanup.js Mock AsyncStorage completely. Tests to write: describe('clearUserSpecificData') - Clears all keys containing the uid - Does NOT clear keys for other uids - AsyncStorage error → caught, does not crash app - Missing uid → no keys cleared, no crash describe('onUserSignOut') - Clears all user-specific cache keys for that uid - Push token cleanup called - AsyncStorage error → caught, sign out still completes - After clear: no prior user data accessible by key describe('onUserSwitch') - Old user's data cleared before new user loads - New user's data not affected - Partial failure on old user clear → still loads new user, logs warning - THIS IS THE PRIVACY TEST: after switch, explicitly check that old user's nutrition and AI chat cache keys return null not old data describe('clearAllUserData') - Clears every app-specific key in AsyncStorage - After clear: all checked keys return null - AsyncStorage error → caught not crash After writing run: npm run test:coverage -- --testPathPattern=dataCacheCleanup

Write it in plain English. No jargon without explanation. Format it clearly with sections and bullet points. Think of it as a reference I can study from. --- ## Sections to cover ### 1. What the product does One paragraph. What problem does CoachConnect solve, who are the two user types, and what does each one do. ### 2. High level architecture - How many main layers does the system have - What runs on the client (mobile app) - What runs on the server (backend) - What runs on Firebase - How do they communicate with each other - Draw a simple ASCII diagram showing the layers ### 3. Frontend — React Native mobile app - What framework and why (Expo SDK 54) - How navigation is structured (two separate app shells — ClientApp, TrainerApp) - How the app decides which shell to show (AuthGate) - What state management approach is used - How the app communicates with the backend - Key screens and what they do (one line each) ### 4. Backend — Express on Google Cloud Run - What the server is responsible for - Why Cloud Run was chosen (serverless, scales to zero) - How authentication works (Firebase Auth tokens verified on every request) - What the main route groups are and what each covers - How AI requests are handled (DeepSeek for chat, Claude for workout generation) - How food search works end to end (Serper → USDA FDC → Open Food Facts fallback chain) ### 5. Database — Firebase Firestore - Why Firestore was chosen - Key collections and what they store (users, nutrition_logs, dailyLogs, trainer_clients, trainer_client_links, aiChats, workoutPlans, conversations, notes) - The 3-surface trainer-client link and why it exists (users.trainerId + trainer_clients + trainer_client_links must always stay in sync) - How real-time listeners are used vs one-time reads - What data lives in AsyncStorage vs Firestore and why ### 6. AI systems - AI Coach: what model, what it can do, how tool calling works, how context is built, what the 15 tools are grouped by category - Workout generation: what model, how the prompt is built, how the response is parsed, how the monthly limit is enforced - How the client and server guard against false-positive tool confirmations - How hallucination is prevented in tool calls ### 7. Payments — Stripe Connect - Two revenue streams explained simply (trainer subscription + platform fee) - How Stripe Connect Express accounts work for trainer payouts - How the 5% platform fee is taken automatically - What happens if a trainer's subscription lapses ### 8. Authentication flow - How sign up works (email or Google) - How onboarding works (role selection, trainer invite code, profile setup) - How AuthGate routes users after login (normalizeAppRole, profileNeedsOnboarding) - How trainer-client linking works end to end ### 9. Key technical decisions and tradeoffs For each decision explain what was chosen, what the alternative was, and why this was better: - Expo over bare React Native - Firebase over a custom database - Cloud Run over a traditional server - DeepSeek over OpenAI for AI Coach - Stripe Connect over manual payouts - Serper over direct Google Search API ### 10. How I would scale this If CoachConnect had 10,000 trainers: - What would break first - What would need to change in the backend - What would need

- [41] `line:414` `kind:user_query_tag` Fix the following server-side security issues in CoachConnect. Read every file completely before changing anything. Only change what is listed. --- ## Fix 1 — Wire AI Coach message limits Files: - server/index.js (find handleAICoachRequest around line 2247+) - server/index.js (find enforceDailyMessageLimit around lines 1438-1455) THE BUG: enforceDailyMessageLimit exists but is never called. Production has limits disabled by default. Fix Part A — Call the limit function: At the very start of handleAICoachRequest before any DeepSeek call add: const limitResult = await enforceDailyMessageLimit( userId, db ); if (limitResult.blocked) { return res.status(429).json({ error: 'Daily message limit reached', resetAt: limitResult.resetAt }); } Fix Part B — Default limits to ON: In server/deploy.sh and scripts/syncCloudRunEnv.sh find AI_COACH_ENFORCE_LIMITS and change default to 1 (enabled). If the variable doesn't exist in the env sync script add it with value 1. --- ## Fix 2 — Gate test suite header to non-production only Files: - server/index.js (lines 208-230 and 256-261) - server/routes/userRoutes.js (lines 81-95) THE BUG: Any user can send X-AI-Coach-Test-Suite: 1 header to bypass rate limits and reset usage in production. Fix: Wrap every check for this header in a production guard: Change from: if (req.headers['x-ai-coach-test-suite'] === '1') To: if (process.env.NODE_ENV !== 'production' && req.headers['x-ai-coach-test-suite'] === '1') Apply to every place this header is checked in server/index.js and userRoutes.js. Also make the reset-usage endpoint return 404 in production: if (process.env.NODE_ENV === 'production') { return res.status(404).json({ error: 'Not found' }); } --- ## Fix 3 — Protect invite code linking without breaking it File: server/routes/onboardingRoutes.js Location: POST /api/onboarding/complete (lines 211-244) CONTEXT: There are two connection flows in CoachConnect: Flow 1 — Invite code during onboarding: Client enters a 6-character trainer invite code. Server validates it via validateTrainerCode. This is intentional consent — trainer gave out the code. Keep this flow exactly as is. Flow 2 — Marketplace request after onboarding: This goes through messages with status pending then trainer accepts via TrainerMarketplaceModal. This flow is separate from onboarding. THE BUG: The invite code flow directly links without checking if the code actually belongs to a real active trainer. It only checks role === 'trainer' exists. A valid trainer UID with no invite code match could still be linked. Fix: Keep the invite code flow intact but add one additional validation step: Before writing the 3-surface link during onboarding completion, verify: 1. The trainerId exists in users collection with role === 'trainer' (already done) 2. AND the trainer's inviteCode field on their users doc matches the code the client entered during onboarding (new check) If the code doesn't match the trainer's actual inviteCode field: - Skip the link writes - Return { success: true, trainerLinked: false, reason: 'invite_code_mismatch' } - Still complete onboarding — just without the link Do NOT add marketplace request validation here. Marketplace connections happen post-onboarding via a separate flow. --- ## Fix 4 — Wrap onboarding link writes in a batch File: server/lib/onboardingCompleteLinks.js Location: lines 6-30 THE BUG: Three separate Firestore writes can partially fail leaving the database in a broken half-linked state: - trainer_client_links/{trainerId}_{clientId} - trainer_clients/{trainerId}/clients/{clientId} - users/{clientId} trainerId field Fix: Wrap all three writes in a single Firestore batch so they all succeed or all fail together: const batch = db.batch(); batch.set(userRef, userPayload, { merge: true }); batch.set(crmRef, crmPayload, { merge: true }); batch.set(linkRef, linkPayload, { merge: true }); await batch.commit(); --- ## Fix 5 — Block deleteLog without confirmation token File: server/routes/aiCoachRoutes.js Location: lines 20-24 and 48-50 THE BUG: deleteLog executes if confirmed: true is in the request body. A script could bulk-delete all logs. Fix: Require a short-lived token instead of a boolean. Add an in-memory pending confirmations store at the top of the file: const pendingConfirmations = new Map(); On tool proposal generate a token: const token = crypto.randomUUID(); pendingConfirmations.set(token, { userId, logId, expiresAt: Date.now() + 300000 }); Return the token to the client with the proposal. On execute validate the token: const pending = pendingConfirmations.get(token); if (!pending || pending.userId !== userId || pending.expiresAt {}}. Replace the no-op with a real handler that: 1. Calls getOrCreateConversation(clientId, trainerId) from trainerMessaging.js 2. Then calls sendClientRequest with: - conversationId from step 1 - senderId: clientId - recipientId: trainerId - requestType: 'connection' - clientIntro: the intro text from the modal - client metadata: goals, experience, equipment In ClientApp.js wire the imported sendClientRequest to this handler. After the request is sent: - Show a success toast: "Request sent to [trainer name]" - Close the profile sheet - The client waits for trainer to accept in their Client Requests tab --- After all fixes run: npm test All existing tests must still pass. Report any test that fails and why.
  - Context screenshots: /Users/captainchris20/.cursor/projects/Users-captainchris20-Desktop-My-Coding-Portfolio-7th-Project-Coach-Connect-Mobile-App/assets/Screenshot_2026-06-11_at_11.58.33_PM-9b438f42-90c8-46b1-8553-85eaebc24041.png | /Users/captainchris20/.cursor/projects/Users-captainchris20-Desktop-My-Coding-Portfolio-7th-Project-Coach-Connect-Mobile-App/assets/Screenshot_2026-06-12_at_12.02.40_AM-2d21df36-43ec-4203-bdf0-a031d4ce77a7.png | /Users/captainchris20/.cursor/projects/Users-captainchris20-Desktop-My-Coding-Portfolio-7th-Project-Coach-Connect-Mobile-App/assets/Screenshot_2026-06-12_at_12.02.48_AM-3a358a13-884d-4e09-b857-ab51486e65d3.png

- [44] `line:469` `kind:user_query_tag` Fix the AI Coach daily message limit in CoachConnect. Read these files completely before changing anything: - server/index.js (find enforceDailyMessageLimit) - server/config/apiCosts.js (find USER_LIMITS) - src/ai/services/deepseekService.js - src/aiChat/screens/AIChatScreen.jsx (find where isError limit message is shown) --- ## Fix 1 — Raise and tier the daily limit File: server/index.js Function: enforceDailyMessageLimit THE PROBLEM: Limit is hardcoded to 10 which is too low for real coaching use. DeepSeek is cheap enough to support much higher limits. Fix: Replace the hardcoded 10 with a role-based limit pulled from the user's Firestore profile: Before checking the count read the user's role: const userDoc = await db .collection('users') .doc(userId) .get(); const role = userDoc.data()?.role || 'client'; const hasTrainer = !!userDoc.data()?.trainerId; Set limits based on role: - role === 'trainer' → 100 messages/day - role === 'client' && hasTrainer → 50 messages/day - role === 'client' && !hasTrainer → 30 messages/day Replace the hardcoded limit = 10 with this role-based value. Also update the server startup log to reflect the new tiered limits instead of "(10/day)". --- ## Fix 2 — Show reset time to the user Files: - src/ai/services/deepseekService.js (find where 429 response is handled) - src/aiChat/screens/AIChatScreen.jsx (find where limit error bubble is shown) THE PROBLEM: When a user hits the limit they see: "Daily message limit reached" with no information on when it resets. resetAt is returned by the API but never displayed. Fix: Update the error message shown in the chat bubble to include the reset time: Parse resetAt from the API response. Format it as a readable local time: const resetTime = new Date(resetAt) .toLocaleTimeString([], { hour: '2-digit', minute: '2-digit' }); Show this message instead: "You've reached your daily AI Coach limit. Resets at [resetTime] tonight." If resetAt is not available fall back to: "You've reached your daily AI Coach limit. #!/bin/bash BASE_URL=${1:-"http://localhost:8080"} TIMESTAMP=$(date +"%Y-%m-%d_%H-%M-%S") RESULTS_FILE="load-tests/results/run_${TIMESTAMP}.txt" mkdir -p load-tests/results echo "CoachConnect Load Test Results" \ > $RESULTS_FILE echo "==============================" \ >> $RESULTS_FILE echo "Timestamp: $(date)" >> $RESULTS_FILE echo "Target: $BASE_URL" >> $RESULTS_FILE echo "" >> $RESULTS_FILE echo "All results saved to: $RESULTS_FILE" echo "" run_test() { local TEST_NAME=$1 local TEST_FILE=$2 echo "Running: $TEST_NAME..." echo "=== $TEST_NAME ===" >> $RESULTS_FILE echo "Started: $(date)" >> $RESULTS_FILE echo "" >> $RESULTS_FILE k6 run --env BASE_URL=$BASE_URL \ $TEST_FILE 2>&1 | tee -a $RESULTS_FILE local EXIT_CODE=${PIPESTATUS[0]} echo "" >> $RESULTS_FILE echo "Finished: $(date)" >> $RESULTS_FILE if [ $EXIT_CODE -eq 0 ]; then echo "Result: PASSED" >> $RESULTS_FILE echo "✅ $TEST_NAME PASSED" else echo "Result: FAILED" >> $RESULTS_FILE echo "❌ $TEST_NAME FAILED" fi echo "==============================" \ >> $RESULTS_FILE echo "" >> $RESULTS_FILE } run_test "1 - AI Coach Load Test" \ load-tests/aiCoach.js run_test "2 - Food Search Load Test" \ load-tests/foodSearch.js run_test "3 - Workout Generation (MOCKED)" \ load-tests/workoutGeneration.js run_test "4 - Auth Spike Test" \ load-tests/authSpike.js run_test "5 - Malicious User Test" \ load-tests/maliciousUser.js run_test "6 - Bot Users Test" \ load-tests/botUsers.js run_test "7 - Barcode Spike Test" \ load-tests/barcodeSpike.js run_test "8 - Trainer Dashboard Test" \ load-tests/trainerDashboard.js run_test "9 - Midnight Rollover Test" \ load-tests/midnightRollover.js run_test "10 - Long Coach Session Test" \ load-tests/longCoachSession.js echo "" >> $RESULTS_FILE echo "==============================" \ >> $RESULTS_FILE echo "SUMMARY" >> $RESULTS_FILE echo "==============================" \ >> $RESULTS_FILE echo "All tests complete: $(date)" \ >> $RESULTS_FILE echo "Results file: $RESULTS_FILE" \ >> $RESULTS_FILE echo "" >> $RESULTS_FILE echo "NOTE: Cold start test run manually:" \ >> $RESULTS_FILE echo "Scale Cloud Run to 0 instances first" \ >> $RESULTS_FILE echo "Then run:" >> $RESULTS_FILE echo "k6 run --env BASE_URL=$BASE_URL \ load-tests/coldStartRecovery.js" \ >> $RESULTS_FILE echo "" echo "==============================" echo "All tests complete" echo "Results saved to: $RESULTS_FILE" echo "" echo "NOTE: Run cold start test manually:" echo "Scale Cloud Run to 0 instances first" echo "k6 run --env BASE_URL=$BASE_URL \ load-tests/coldStartRecovery.js" --- ## Create load-tests/README.md Include all of the following: How to run all 10 tests and save results: chmod +x load-tests/run-all.sh ./load-tests/run-all.sh http://localhost:8080 How to run against production (read warnings first): ./load-tests/run-all.sh https://cloud-run-url How to run a single test: k6 run --env BASE_URL=http://localhost:8080 load-tests/aiCoach.js Cost warnings: All 10 tests in run-all.sh use fake tokens and mocks — zero API credits spent. DO NOT run with real Firebase tokens unless you want real DeepSeek/Serper calls to fire. workout generation always uses X-Load-Test mock regardless of token — safe in any environment. What each test covers: 1. aiCoach — 100 users, auth and rate limiting 2. foodSearch — 500 users, 65 real restaurant queries 3. workoutGeneration — 50 users, mocked Claude 4. authSpike — 1000 users cold start 5. maliciousUser — rapid fire abuse prevention 6. botUsers — 1000 random users 4 minutes 7. barcodeSpike — 200 users real product barcodes 8. trainerDashboard — 50 trainers client rosters 9. midnightRollover — 300 users at UTC midnight 10. longCoachSession — 10 users 50 messages each 11. coldStartRecovery — manual only, scale to 0 first What passing looks like: All thresholds green in k6 output No 500 status codes anywhere PASSED printed next to each test name in terminal Results file shows Result: PASSED for each test Where results are saved: load-tests/results/run_YYYY-MM-DD_HH-MM-SS.txt One file per run containing all 10 test outputs Each test section shows start time, full k6 output, finish time, and PASSED or FAILED --- ## Update .gitignore Add these lines if not already present: load-tests/results/ load-tests/node_modules/ --- Do not run any tests yet. Create all files including: - All 11 test scripts - run-all.sh - README.md - The mock response in workoutRoutes.js - .gitignore updates Report when done with a list of every file created.
